     1→//! MCP client: manages the protocol handshake and tool interactions with a single MCP server.
     2→
     3→use std::{collections::HashMap, sync::Arc, time::Duration};
     4→
     5→use tracing::{debug, info, warn};
     6→
     7→#[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
     8→use std::time::Instant;
     9→
    10→#[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
    11→use moltis_metrics::{counter, gauge, histogram, labels, mcp as mcp_metrics};
    12→
    13→use crate::{
    14→    auth::SharedAuthProvider,
    15→    error::{Context, Error, Result},
    16→    remote::ResolvedRemoteConfig,
    17→    sse_transport::SseTransport,
    18→    traits::{McpClientTrait, McpTransport},
    19→    transport::StdioTransport,
    20→    types::{
    21→        ClientCapabilities, ClientInfo, InitializeParams, InitializeResult, McpToolDef,
    22→        PROTOCOL_VERSION, ToolsCallParams, ToolsCallResult, ToolsListResult,
    23→    },
    24→};
    25→
    26→/// State of an MCP client connection.
    27→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    28→pub enum McpClientState {
    29→    /// Transport spawned, not yet initialized.
    30→    Connected,
    31→    /// `initialize` completed, `initialized` notification sent.
    32→    Ready,
    33→    /// OAuth authentication in progress (waiting for browser).
    34→    Authenticating,
    35→    /// Server process exited or was shut down.
    36→    Closed,
    37→}
    38→
    39→/// An MCP client connected to a single server via stdio.
    40→pub struct McpClient {
    41→    server_name: String,
    42→    transport: Arc<dyn McpTransport>,
    43→    state: McpClientState,
    44→    server_info: Option<InitializeResult>,
    45→    tools: Vec<McpToolDef>,
    46→}
    47→
    48→impl McpClient {
    49→    /// Spawn the server process and perform the MCP handshake (initialize + initialized).
    50→    pub async fn connect(
    51→        server_name: &str,
    52→        command: &str,
    53→        args: &[String],
    54→        env: &HashMap<String, String>,
    55→        request_timeout: Duration,
    56→    ) -> Result<Self> {
    57→        info!(server = %server_name, command = %command, args = ?args, "connecting to MCP server");
    58→        let transport =
    59→            StdioTransport::spawn_with_timeout(command, args, env, request_timeout).await?;
    60→
    61→        let mut client = Self {
    62→            server_name: server_name.into(),
    63→            transport,
    64→            state: McpClientState::Connected,
    65→            server_info: None,
    66→            tools: Vec::new(),
    67→        };
    68→
    69→        if let Err(e) = client.initialize().await {
    70→            warn!(server = %server_name, error = %e, "MCP initialize handshake failed");
    71→            return Err(e);
    72→        }
    73→
    74→        // Track MCP server connection
    75→        #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
    76→        {
    77→            counter!(mcp_metrics::SERVER_CONNECTIONS_TOTAL, labels::SERVER => server_name.to_string())
    78→                .increment(1);
    79→            gauge!(mcp_metrics::SERVERS_CONNECTED).increment(1.0);
    80→        }
    81→
    82→        Ok(client)
    83→    }
    84→
    85→    /// Connect to a remote MCP server over HTTP/SSE or Streamable HTTP.
    86→    pub async fn connect_sse(
    87→        server_name: &str,
    88→        remote: &ResolvedRemoteConfig,
    89→        request_timeout: Duration,
    90→    ) -> Result<Self> {
    91→        info!(
    92→            server = %server_name,
    93→            url = %remote.display_url(),
    94→            "connecting to remote MCP server"
    95→        );
    96→        let transport = SseTransport::new_with_remote(remote.clone(), request_timeout)?;
    97→
    98→        let mut client = Self {
    99→            server_name: server_name.into(),
   100→            transport,
   101→            state: McpClientState::Connected,
   102→            server_info: None,
   103→            tools: Vec::new(),
   104→        };
   105→
   106→        if let Err(e) = client.initialize().await {
   107→            warn!(server = %server_name, error = %e, "remote MCP initialize handshake failed");
   108→            return Err(e);
   109→        }
   110→        Ok(client)
   111→    }
   112→
   113→    /// Connect to a remote MCP server over HTTP/SSE or Streamable HTTP with an OAuth auth provider.
   114→    pub async fn connect_sse_with_auth(
   115→        server_name: &str,
   116→        remote: &ResolvedRemoteConfig,
   117→        auth: SharedAuthProvider,
   118→        request_timeout: Duration,
   119→    ) -> Result<Self> {
   120→        info!(
   121→            server = %server_name,
   122→            url = %remote.display_url(),
   123→            "connecting to remote MCP server (with auth)"
   124→        );
   125→        let transport = SseTransport::with_auth_remote(remote.clone(), auth, request_timeout)?;
   126→
   127→        let mut client = Self {
   128→            server_name: server_name.into(),
   129→            transport,
   130→            state: McpClientState::Connected,
   131→            server_info: None,
   132→            tools: Vec::new(),
   133→        };
   134→
   135→        if let Err(e) = client.initialize().await {
   136→            warn!(server = %server_name, error = %e, "MCP SSE (auth) initialize handshake failed");
   137→            return Err(e);
   138→        }
   139→
   140→        #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   141→        {
   142→            counter!(mcp_metrics::SERVER_CONNECTIONS_TOTAL, labels::SERVER => server_name.to_string())
   143→                .increment(1);
   144→            gauge!(mcp_metrics::SERVERS_CONNECTED).increment(1.0);
   145→        }
   146→
   147→        Ok(client)
   148→    }
   149→
   150→    async fn initialize(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
   151→        let params = InitializeParams {
   152→            protocol_version: PROTOCOL_VERSION.into(),
   153→            capabilities: ClientCapabilities::default(),
   154→            client_info: ClientInfo {
   155→                name: "moltis".into(),
   156→                version: moltis_config::VERSION.into(),
   157→            },
   158→        };
   159→
   160→        let resp = self
   161→            .transport
   162→            .request("initialize", Some(serde_json::to_value(&params)?))
   163→            .await
   164→            .context("MCP initialize request failed")?;
   165→
   166→        let result: InitializeResult =
   167→            serde_json::from_value(resp.result.context("MCP initialize returned no result")?)
   168→                .context("failed to parse MCP initialize result")?;
   169→
   170→        info!(
   171→            server = %self.server_name,
   172→            protocol = %result.protocol_version,
   173→            server_name = %result.server_info.name,
   174→            "MCP server initialized"
   175→        );
   176→
   177→        self.server_info = Some(result);
   178→
   179→        // Send `initialized` notification to complete handshake.
   180→        self.transport
   181→            .notify("notifications/initialized", None)
   182→            .await?;
   183→        self.state = McpClientState::Ready;
   184→
   185→        Ok(())
   186→    }
   187→
   188→    fn ensure_ready(&self) -> Result<()> {
   189→        if self.state != McpClientState::Ready {
   190→            return Err(Error::message(format!(
   191→                "MCP client for '{}' is not ready (state: {:?})",
   192→                self.server_name, self.state
   193→            )));
   194→        }
   195→        Ok(())
   196→    }
   197→}
   198→
   199→#[async_trait::async_trait]
   200→impl McpClientTrait for McpClient {
   201→    fn server_name(&self) -> &str {
   202→        &self.server_name
   203→    }
   204→
   205→    fn state(&self) -> McpClientState {
   206→        self.state
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    fn tools(&self) -> &[McpToolDef] {
   210→        &self.tools
   211→    }
   212→
   213→    async fn list_tools(&mut self) -> Result<&[McpToolDef]> {
   214→        self.ensure_ready()?;
   215→
   216→        let resp = self.transport.request("tools/list", None).await?;
   217→        let mut result: ToolsListResult =
   218→            serde_json::from_value(resp.result.context("tools/list returned no result")?)?;
   219→        result
   220→            .tools
   221→            .sort_by(|left, right| left.name.cmp(&right.name));
   222→
   223→        debug!(
   224→            server = %self.server_name,
   225→            count = result.tools.len(),
   226→            "fetched MCP tools"
   227→        );
   228→
   229→        self.tools = result.tools;
   230→        Ok(&self.tools)
   231→    }
   232→
   233→    async fn call_tool(&self, name: &str, arguments: serde_json::Value) -> Result<ToolsCallResult> {
   234→        self.ensure_ready()?;
   235→
   236→        #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   237→        let start = Instant::now();
   238→
   239→        let params = ToolsCallParams {
   240→            name: name.into(),
   241→            arguments,
   242→        };
   243→
   244→        let result = self
   245→            .transport
   246→            .request("tools/call", Some(serde_json::to_value(&params)?))
   247→            .await;
   248→
   249→        match result {
   250→            Ok(resp) => {
   251→                let result: ToolsCallResult =
   252→                    serde_json::from_value(resp.result.context("tools/call returned no result")?)?;
   253→
   254→                #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   255→                {
   256→                    let duration = start.elapsed().as_secs_f64();
   257→
   258→                    counter!(
   259→                        mcp_metrics::TOOL_CALLS_TOTAL,
   260→                        labels::SERVER => self.server_name.clone(),
   261→                        labels::TOOL => name.to_string()
   262→                    )
   263→                    .increment(1);
   264→
   265→                    histogram!(
   266→                        mcp_metrics::TOOL_CALL_DURATION_SECONDS,
   267→                        labels::SERVER => self.server_name.clone(),
   268→                        labels::TOOL => name.to_string()
   269→                    )
   270→                    .record(duration);
   271→                }
   272→
   273→                Ok(result)
   274→            },
   275→            Err(e) => {
   276→                #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   277→                {
   278→                    counter!(
   279→                        mcp_metrics::TOOL_CALL_ERRORS_TOTAL,
   280→                        labels::SERVER => self.server_name.clone(),
   281→                        labels::TOOL => name.to_string()
   282→                    )
   283→                    .increment(1);
   284→                }
   285→                Err(e)
   286→            },
   287→        }
   288→    }
   289→
   290→    async fn is_alive(&self) -> bool {
   291→        self.transport.is_alive().await
   292→    }
   293→
   294→    async fn shutdown(&mut self) {
   295→        self.state = McpClientState::Closed;
   296→        self.transport.kill().await;
   297→
   298→        // Decrement connected servers gauge
   299→        #[cfg(feature = "metrics")]
   300→        {
   301→            gauge!(mcp_metrics::SERVERS_CONNECTED).decrement(1.0);
   302→        }
   303→    }
   304→}
   305→

```
     1→//! Trait abstractions for MCP transport and client layers.
     2→//!
     3→//! These traits allow swapping the underlying MCP implementation (e.g. stdio vs rmcp-sdk)
     4→//! without changing call sites in `manager.rs`, `tool_bridge.rs`, or `mcp_service.rs`.
     5→
     6→use {async_trait::async_trait, serde_json::Value};
     7→
     8→use crate::{
     9→    client::McpClientState,
    10→    error::Result,
    11→    types::{JsonRpcResponse, McpToolDef, ToolsCallResult},
    12→};
    13→
    14→/// Transport layer for MCP communication (JSON-RPC).
    15→///
    16→/// `StdioTransport` implements this over stdin/stdout of a child process.
    17→/// A future HTTP/SSE transport (e.g. via rmcp-sdk) would also implement this trait.
    18→#[async_trait]
    19→pub trait McpTransport: Send + Sync {
    20→    /// Send a JSON-RPC request and wait for the response.
    21→    async fn request(&self, method: &str, params: Option<Value>) -> Result<JsonRpcResponse>;
    22→
    23→    /// Send a JSON-RPC notification (no response expected).
    24→    async fn notify(&self, method: &str, params: Option<Value>) -> Result<()>;
    25→
    26→    /// Check if the underlying connection/process is still alive.
    27→    async fn is_alive(&self) -> bool;
    28→
    29→    /// Kill/close the underlying connection/process.
    30→    async fn kill(&self);
    31→}
    32→
    33→/// Client-level abstraction for an MCP server connection.
    34→///
    35→/// `McpClient` implements this over `StdioTransport`. A future `RmcpClient`
    36→/// wrapper would also implement this trait.
    37→#[async_trait]
    38→pub trait McpClientTrait: Send + Sync {
    39→    /// The display name of the connected server.
    40→    fn server_name(&self) -> &str;
    41→
    42→    /// Current connection state.
    43→    fn state(&self) -> McpClientState;
    44→
    45→    /// Cached tool definitions (call `list_tools` first to populate).
    46→    fn tools(&self) -> &[McpToolDef];
    47→
    48→    /// Fetch the list of tools from the server, caching the result.
    49→    async fn list_tools(&mut self) -> Result<&[McpToolDef]>;
    50→
    51→    /// Call a tool on the server.
    52→    async fn call_tool(&self, name: &str, arguments: Value) -> Result<ToolsCallResult>;
    53→
    54→    /// Check if the server process/connection is still alive.
    55→    async fn is_alive(&self) -> bool;
    56→
    57→    /// Shut down the server connection.
    58→    async fn shutdown(&mut self);
    59→}
    60→
```

     1→// Shared Playwright test fixture with automatic error-context capture.
     2→//
     3→// Every spec file should import { test, expect } from this module instead of
     4→// from "@playwright/test".  On failure the fixture attaches a markdown
     5→// snapshot of every open page (URL, title, visible text) so CI logs contain
     6→// enough context to diagnose failures without downloading trace artifacts.
     7→
     8→const { test: base, expect } = require("@playwright/test");
     9→
    10→var test = base.extend({
    11→	page: async ({ page, context }, use, testInfo) => {
    12→		await use(page);
    13→
    14→		if (testInfo.status !== testInfo.expectedStatus) {
    15→			var pages = context.pages();
    16→			var parts = [];
    17→
    18→			for (var i = 0; i < pages.length; i++) {
    19→				var p = pages[i];
    20→				try {
    21→					if (p.isClosed()) {
    22→						parts.push(`### Page ${i + 1}: (closed)`);
    23→						continue;
    24→					}
    25→					var url = p.url();
    26→					var title = await p.title().catch(() => "(unknown)");
    27→					var text = await p
    28→						.evaluate(() => document.body?.innerText?.slice(0, 3000) || "")
    29→						.catch(() => "(unavailable)");
    30→					parts.push(`### Page ${i + 1}: ${title}\n- **URL**: ${url}\n\n\`\`\`\n${text}\n\`\`\``);
    31→				} catch {
    32→					parts.push(`### Page ${i + 1}: (error reading page)`);
    33→				}
    34→			}
    35→
    36→			var md = ["## Error Context", "", `**Test**: ${testInfo.title}`, `**Status**: ${testInfo.status}`, ""]
    37→				.concat(parts)
    38→				.join("\n");
    39→
    40→			await testInfo.attach("error-context", {
    41→				body: Buffer.from(md, "utf-8"),
    42→				contentType: "text/markdown",
    43→			});
    44→		}
    45→	},
    46→});
    47→
    48→module.exports = { test, expect };
    49→
```

     1→const { expect } = require("@playwright/test");
     2→
     3→/**
     4→ * Wait until the SPA has mounted visible content into #pageContent.
     5→ * This is a stable cross-route readiness signal for the app shell.
     6→ */
     7→async function expectPageContentMounted(page) {
     8→	await expect
     9→		.poll(
    10→			async () => {
    11→				try {
    12→					return await page.evaluate(() => {
    13→						const el = document.getElementById("pageContent");
    14→						if (!el) return 0;
    15→						return el.childElementCount;
    16→					});
    17→				} catch (error) {
    18→					if (isRetryableNavigationError(error)) return 0;
    19→					throw error;
    20→				}
    21→			},
    22→			{
    23→				timeout: 20_000,
    24→			},
    25→		)
    26→		.toBeGreaterThan(0);
    27→}
    28→
    29→/**
    30→ * Collect uncaught page errors for later assertion.
    31→ * Returns an array that fills as errors occur.
    32→ *
    33→ * Usage:
    34→ *   const pageErrors = watchPageErrors(page);
    35→ *   // ... interact with page ...
    36→ *   expect(pageErrors).toEqual([]);
    37→ */
    38→function watchPageErrors(page) {
    39→	const pageErrors = [];
    40→	page.on("pageerror", (err) => pageErrors.push(err.message));
    41→	return pageErrors;
    42→}
    43→
    44→/**
    45→ * Wait for the WebSocket connection status dot to reach "connected".
    46→ * Note: #statusText is intentionally set to "" when connected, so we
    47→ * only check the dot's CSS class.
    48→ */
    49→async function waitForWsConnected(page, timeoutMs = 20_000) {
    50→	await expect
    51→		.poll(
    52→			async () => {
    53→				const statusDotConnected = await page
    54→					.locator("#statusDot")
    55→					.getAttribute("class")
    56→					.then((cls) => /connected/.test(cls || ""))
    57→					.catch(() => false);
    58→				if (!statusDotConnected) return false;
    59→				return page
    60→					.evaluate(async () => {
    61→						const appScript = document.querySelector('script[type="module"][src*="js/app.js"]');
    62→						if (!appScript) return false;
    63→						const appUrl = new URL(appScript.src, window.location.origin);
    64→						const prefix = appUrl.href.slice(0, appUrl.href.length - "js/app.js".length);
    65→						const state = await import(`${prefix}js/state.js`);
    66→						return Boolean(state.connected && state.ws && state.ws.readyState === WebSocket.OPEN);
    67→					})
    68→					.catch(() => false);
    69→			},
    70→			{ timeout: timeoutMs },
    71→		)
    72→		.toBe(true);
    73→}
    74→
    75→function isRetryableNavigationError(error) {
    76→	var message = error?.message || String(error || "");
    77→	return (
    78→		message.includes("net::ERR_ABORTED") ||
    79→		message.includes("Execution context was destroyed") ||
    80→		message.includes("Target page, context or browser has been closed")
    81→	);
    82→}
    83→
    84→/**
    85→ * Navigate to a path, wait for SPA content to mount, and assert no errors.
    86→ * Returns the pageErrors array for further assertions.
    87→ */
    88→async function navigateAndWait(page, path) {
    89→	const pageErrors = watchPageErrors(page);
    90→	let lastError = null;
    91→	for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 3; attempt++) {
    92→		try {
    93→			await page.goto(path, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });
    94→			await expectPageContentMounted(page);
    95→			return pageErrors;
    96→		} catch (error) {
    97→			lastError = error;
    98→			if (!isRetryableNavigationError(error) || attempt === 2) {
    99→				break;
   100→			}
   101→		}
   102→	}
   103→	if (lastError) throw lastError;
   104→	return pageErrors;
   105→}
   106→
   107→/**
   108→ * Create a new session by clicking the new-session button.
   109→ * Waits for the active key to change, URL to update, and content to mount.
   110→ *
   111→ * Note: we intentionally do NOT wait for the session to appear in the
   112→ * sessions list (store.getByKey). The list is populated asynchronously
   113→ * by the sessions.switch RPC and can be slow under heavy test load.
   114→ * The key change + URL match + page mount are sufficient to prove the
   115→ * session was created; individual tests can wait for store indexing
   116→ * if their assertions require it.
   117→ */
   118→async function createSession(page) {
   119→	const timeoutMs = 20_000;
   120→	const previousActiveKey = await page.evaluate(() => {
   121→		return window.__moltis_stores?.sessionStore?.activeSessionKey?.value || "";
   122→	});
   123→
   124→	await page.locator("#newSessionBtn").click();
   125→	await expect
   126→		.poll(
   127→			() =>
   128→				page.evaluate(() => {
   129→					return window.__moltis_stores?.sessionStore?.activeSessionKey?.value || "";
   130→				}),
   131→			{ timeout: timeoutMs },
   132→		)
   133→		.not.toBe(previousActiveKey);
   134→
   135→	await expect
   136→		.poll(
   137→			() =>
   138→				page.evaluate(() => {
   139→					const key = window.__moltis_stores?.sessionStore?.activeSessionKey?.value || "";
   140→					if (!key) return false;
   141→					return window.location.pathname === `/chats/${key.replace(/:/g, "/")}`;
   142→				}),
   143→			{ timeout: timeoutMs },
   144→		)
   145→		.toBe(true);
   146→
   147→	await expectPageContentMounted(page);
   148→}
   149→
   150→/**
   151→ * Open the chat "More controls" modal and wait for it to be visible.
   152→ */
   153→async function openChatMoreModal(page) {
   154→	const modal = page.locator("#chatMoreModal");
   155→	if (await modal.isVisible().catch(() => false)) return modal;
   156→	await expect(page.locator("#chatMoreBtn")).toBeVisible({ timeout: 10_000 });
   157→	await page.locator("#chatMoreBtn").click();
   158→	await expect(modal).toBeVisible({ timeout: 10_000 });
   159→	return modal;
   160→}
   161→
   162→/**
   163→ * Close the chat "More controls" modal by clicking the backdrop.
   164→ */
   165→async function closeChatMoreModal(page) {
   166→	const modal = page.locator("#chatMoreModal");
   167→	if (!(await modal.isVisible().catch(() => false))) return;
   168→	await modal.click({ position: { x: 8, y: 8 } });
   169→	await expect(modal).toBeHidden({ timeout: 10_000 });
   170→}
   171→
   172→module.exports = {
   173→	expectPageContentMounted,
   174→	watchPageErrors,
   175→	waitForWsConnected,
   176→	navigateAndWait,
   177→	createSession,
   178→	openChatMoreModal,
   179→	closeChatMoreModal,
   180→};
   181→

     1→const { defineConfig } = require("@playwright/test");
     2→const { execFileSync } = require("node:child_process");
     3→
     4→function pickFreePort() {
     5→	return execFileSync(
     6→		process.execPath,
     7→		[
     8→			"-e",
     9→			"const net=require('net');const s=net.createServer();s.listen(0,'127.0.0.1',()=>{process.stdout.write(String(s.address().port));s.close();});",
    10→		],
    11→		{ encoding: "utf8" },
    12→	).trim();
    13→}
    14→
    15→function resolvePort(envVar, usedPortSet) {
    16→	var configured = process.env[envVar];
    17→	if (configured && configured !== "0") {
    18→		usedPortSet.add(configured);
    19→		return configured;
    20→	}
    21→	var picked = pickFreePort();
    22→	while (usedPortSet.has(picked)) {
    23→		picked = pickFreePort();
    24→	}
    25→	process.env[envVar] = picked;
    26→	usedPortSet.add(picked);
    27→	return picked;
    28→}
    29→

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Start the mock OAuth server + gateway with OAuth config overrides.
# The mock server's /authorize and /token endpoints replace the real
# OpenAI Codex OAuth endpoints, letting us test the full browser-side
# OAuth PKCE flow without any external dependencies.

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
REPO_ROOT="$(cd -- "${SCRIPT_DIR}/../../../.." && pwd)"

PORT="${MOLTIS_E2E_OAUTH_PORT:-0}"
RUNTIME_ROOT="${MOLTIS_E2E_OAUTH_RUNTIME_DIR:-${REPO_ROOT}/target/e2e-runtime-oauth}"
CONFIG_DIR="${RUNTIME_ROOT}/config"
DATA_DIR="${RUNTIME_ROOT}/data"
HOME_DIR="${RUNTIME_ROOT}/home"

rm -rf "${RUNTIME_ROOT}"
mkdir -p "${CONFIG_DIR}" "${DATA_DIR}" "${HOME_DIR}/.config" "${HOME_DIR}/.codex"

# Seed identity files so the app skips onboarding
cat > "${DATA_DIR}/IDENTITY.md" <<'EOF'
---
name: e2e-bot
---

# IDENTITY.md

This file is managed by Moltis settings.
EOF

cat > "${DATA_DIR}/USER.md" <<'EOF'
---
name: e2e-user
---

# USER.md

This file is managed by Moltis settings.
EOF

# Mark onboarding as complete so the app skips the wizard.
touch "${DATA_DIR}/.onboarded"

# Start mock OAuth server and capture its port
MOCK_PORT_FILE=$(mktemp)
node "${SCRIPT_DIR}/mock-oauth-server.js" > "${MOCK_PORT_FILE}" &
MOCK_PID=$!

# Wait for the mock server to print its port (up to 5 seconds)
for i in $(seq 1 50); do
	if [ -s "${MOCK_PORT_FILE}" ]; then
		break
	fi
	sleep 0.1
done

if [ ! -s "${MOCK_PORT_FILE}" ]; then
	echo "ERROR: mock OAuth server did not start" >&2
	kill "${MOCK_PID}" 2>/dev/null || true
	exit 1
fi

MOCK_PORT=$(node -e "process.stdout.write(String(JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync('${MOCK_PORT_FILE}','utf8').trim()).port))")
echo "Mock OAuth server running on port ${MOCK_PORT}" >&2

# Write the mock port for the test spec to read
echo "${MOCK_PORT}" > "${RUNTIME_ROOT}/mock-oauth-port"

# Clean up the mock server when this script exits
cleanup() {
	kill "${MOCK_PID}" 2>/dev/null || true
	rm -f "${MOCK_PORT_FILE}"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

cd "${REPO_ROOT}"

export MOLTIS_CONFIG_DIR="${CONFIG_DIR}"
export MOLTIS_DATA_DIR="${DATA_DIR}"
export MOLTIS_SERVER__PORT="${PORT}"
export HOME="${HOME_DIR}"
export XDG_CONFIG_HOME="${HOME_DIR}/.config"

# Override OAuth config for openai-codex to point at the mock server.
# Clear the redirect_uri so the gateway's /auth/callback is used instead of
# spawning a local CallbackServer on port 1455 (the upstream-registered URI).
# This lets the e2e test observe token-exchange errors in the popup because
# the gateway completes the exchange synchronously before responding.
export MOLTIS_OAUTH_OPENAI_CODEX_AUTH_URL="http://127.0.0.1:${MOCK_PORT}/authorize"
export MOLTIS_OAUTH_OPENAI_CODEX_TOKEN_URL="http://127.0.0.1:${MOCK_PORT}/token"
export MOLTIS_OAUTH_OPENAI_CODEX_CLIENT_ID="test-client-id"
export MOLTIS_OAUTH_OPENAI_CODEX_REDIRECT_URI=""
# Ensure the Add LLM picker shows the OpenAI Codex provider in this e2e project.
export MOLTIS_PROVIDERS__OFFERED='["openai-codex","openai","github-copilot"]'

# Prefer a pre-built binary to avoid recompiling every test run.
BINARY="${MOLTIS_BINARY:-}"
if [ -z "${BINARY}" ]; then
	for candidate in target/debug/moltis target/release/moltis; do
		if [ -x "${candidate}" ] && { [ -z "${BINARY}" ] || [ "${candidate}" -nt "${BINARY}" ]; }; then
			BINARY="${candidate}"
		fi
	done
fi

if [ -n "${BINARY}" ]; then
	exec "${BINARY}" --no-tls --bind 127.0.0.1 --port "${PORT}"
else
	exec cargo run --bin moltis -- --no-tls --bind 127.0.0.1 --port "${PORT}"
fi
```

    27→	return res.ok;

{
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/zesty-variety/crates/web/ui/e2e/mock-mcp-server.js",
  "content": "// Mock MCP Streamable HTTP server for E2E testing.\n// Implements the MCP protocol over HTTP POST (JSON-RPC 2.0).\n// Validates Bearer token auth when configured.\n// Returns 405 for GET by default (many real Streamable HTTP servers do this).\n//\n// Usage: node mock-mcp-server.js [--bearer-token TOKEN] [--get-status CODE]\n// Prints JSON to stdout: { \"port\": <number> }\n\nconst http = require(\"node:http\");\n\nvar bearerToken = null;\nvar getStatusCode = 405;\nvar tools = [\n\t{\n\t\tname: \"mock_echo\",\n\t\tdescription: \"Echoes the input back\",\n\t\tinputSchema: { type: \"object\", properties: { message: { type: \"string\" } } },\n\t},\n];\n\n// Parse CLI args\nfor (var i = 2; i < process.argv.length; i++) {\n\tif (process.argv[i] === \"--bearer-token\" && process.argv[i + 1]) {\n\t\tbearerToken = process.argv[++i];\n\t}\n\tif (process.argv[i] === \"--get-status\" && process.argv[i + 1]) {\n\t\tgetStatusCode = parseInt(process.argv[++i], 10);\n\t}\n}\n\nfunction respond(res, status, body, extraHeaders) {\n\tvar json = JSON.stringify(body);\n\tvar headers = { \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\", ...extraHeaders };\n\tres.writeHead(status, headers);\n\tres.end(json);\n}\n\nfunction parseBody(req) {\n\treturn new Promise((resolve) => {\n\t\tvar chunks = [];\n\t\treq.on(\"data\", (c) => chunks.push(c));\n\t\treq.on(\"end\", () => {\n\t\t\tvar body = Buffer.concat(chunks).toString();\n\t\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t\tresolve(JSON.parse(body));\n\t\t\t} catch {\n\t\t\t\tresolve(null);\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t});\n\t});\n}\n\nfunction checkAuth(req, res) {\n\tif (!bearerToken) return true;\n\tvar auth = req.headers[\"authorization\"] || \"\";\n\tif (auth === `Bearer ${bearerToken}`) return true;\n\trespond(res, 401, { error: \"unauthorized\" }, { \"WWW-Authenticate\": 'Bearer realm=\"mock-mcp\"' });\n\treturn false;\n}\n\nfunction handleJsonRpc(rpcBody) {\n\tvar method = rpcBody.method;\n\tvar id = rpcBody.id;\n\n\tif (method === \"initialize\") {\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tjsonrpc: \"2.0\",\n\t\t\tid,\n\t\t\tresult: {\n\t\t\t\tprotocolVersion: \"2025-03-26\",\n\t\t\t\tcapabilities: { tools: {} },\n\t\t\t\tserverInfo: { name: \"mock-mcp-server\", version: \"1.0.0\" },\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\n\tif (method === \"notifications/initialized\") {\n\t\t// Notification — no response needed, but we send 202 from caller\n\t\treturn null;\n\t}\n\n\tif (method === \"tools/list\") {\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tjsonrpc: \"2.0\",\n\t\t\tid,\n\t\t\tresult: { tools },\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\n\tif (method === \"tools/call\") {\n\t\tvar args = rpcBody.params?.arguments || {};\n\t\treturn {\n\t\t\tjsonrpc: \"2.0\",\n\t\t\tid,\n\t\t\tresult: {\n\t\t\t\tcontent: [{ type: \"text\", text: `echo: ${args.message || \"(empty)\"}` }],\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t};\n\t}\n\n\treturn {\n\t\tjsonrpc: \"2.0\",\n\t\tid,\n\t\terror: { code: -32601, message: `Method not found: ${method}` },\n\t};\n}\n\nvar server = http.createServer(async (req, res) => {\n\t// GET: health check probe from Moltis. Many real Streamable HTTP servers\n\t// return 405 here because GET is optional in the MCP spec.\n\tif (req.method === \"GET\") {\n\t\tif (req.url === \"/health\") {\n\t\t\treturn respond(res, 200, { ok: true });\n\t\t}\n\t\tres.writeHead(getStatusCode, { Allow: \"POST, DELETE\" });\n\t\tres.end();\n\t\treturn;\n\t}\n\n\t// DELETE: session termination\n\tif (req.method === \"DELETE\") {\n\t\tres.writeHead(204);\n\t\tres.end();\n\t\treturn;\n\t}\n\n\t// POST: JSON-RPC requests\n\tif (req.method === \"POST\") {\n\t\tif (!checkAuth(req, res)) return;\n\n\t\tvar body = await parseBody(req);\n\t\tif (!body || !body.jsonrpc) {\n\t\t\treturn respond(res, 400, { error: \"invalid JSON-RPC request\" });\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar rpcResponse = handleJsonRpc(body);\n\t\tif (rpcResponse === null) {\n\t\t\t// Notification — no response body\n\t\t\tres.writeHead(202);\n\t\t\tres.end();\n\t\t\treturn;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn respond(res, 200, rpcResponse);\n\t}\n\n\tres.writeHead(405, { Allow: \"GET, POST, DELETE\" });\n\tres.end();\n});\n\nserver.listen(0, \"127.0.0.1\", () => {\n\tvar port = server.address().port;\n\tprocess.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify({ port })}\\n`);\n});\n"
}

A 405 response proves the server is reachable and alive.\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_is_alive_405_method_not_allowed_returns_true() {\n        let mut server = mockito::Server::new_async().await;\n        let _mock = server\n            .mock(\"GET\", \"/\")\n            .with_status(405)\n            .with_header(\"allow\", \"POST, DELETE\")\n            .create_async()\n            .await;\n\n        let transport = SseTransport::new(&server.url()).unwrap();\n        // BUG (issue #732): currently returns false because is_alive()\n        // only treats 2xx and 401 as alive. This causes \"dead\" status\n        // in the UI for working Streamable HTTP servers.\n        //\n        // After fix: this assertion should be `assert!(transport.is_alive().await);`\n        assert!(\n            !transport.is_alive().await,\n            \"expected false (unfixed bug #732); flip to assert!(true) when fixed\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// A 403 Forbidden from the health check still proves the server is\n    /// reachable — it just refused the request. The server is alive.\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_is_alive_403_forbidden_returns_true() {\n        let mut server = mockito::Server::new_async().await;\n        let _mock = server\n            .mock(\"GET\", \"/\")\n            .with_status(403)\n            .create_async()\n            .await;\n\n        let transport = SseTransport::new(&server.url()).unwrap();\n        // BUG (issue #732): currently returns false.\n        assert!(\n            !transport.is_alive().await,\n            \"expected false (unfixed bug #732); flip to assert!(true) when fixed\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// A 400 Bad Request still proves the server endpoint is alive.\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_is_alive_400_bad_request_returns_true() {\n        let mut server = mockito::Server::new_async().await;\n        let _mock = server\n            .mock(\"GET\", \"/\")\n            .with_status(400)\n            .with_body(r#\"{\"error\":\"bad request\"}\"#)\n            .create_async()\n            .await;\n\n        let transport = SseTransport::new(&server.url()).unwrap();\n        // BUG (issue #732): currently returns false.\n        assert!(\n            !transport.is_alive().await,\n            \"expected false (unfixed bug #732); flip to assert!(true) when fixed\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_sse_transport_kill_sends_delete_with_session_id() {"

    22→				}
    23→			} catch {
    24→				// Not complete JSON yet, keep accumulating
    25→			}
    26→		});

    29→		child.on("exit", (code) => {
    30→			if (!output) reject(new Error(`Mock MCP server exited with code ${code}`));
    31→		});
    32→
    33→		setTimeout(() => reject(new Error("Mock MCP server startup timeout")), 5000);
    34→	});

> AGENT

All 93 tests pass. Now let me provide the complete analysis and fix plan.

---

## Summary

### Root Cause Analysis

The bug is in `crates/mcp/src/sse_transport.rs:459-495` — the `SseTransport::is_alive()` method:

```rust
async fn is_alive(&self) -> bool {
    // ... sends GET request ...
    match req.send().await {
        Ok(resp) => {
            self.store_session_id_from_response(&resp).await;
            if resp.status() == reqwest::StatusCode::UNAUTHORIZED {
                return true;  // 401 → alive ✓
            }
            resp.status().is_success()  // only 2xx → alive ✗ BUG
        },
        Err(_) => false,  // network error → dead ✓
    }
}
```

The health check sends a **GET** request, but:
- **GET is optional** in the MCP Streamable HTTP spec — servers may only support POST
- Servers return **405 Method Not Allowed**, **403 Forbidden**, or **400 Bad Request** for GET
- The code only treats **2xx** and **401** as "alive"
- Any other HTTP status → `false` → status shows **"dead"** in the UI

This is called from `manager.rs:424-429`:
```rust
McpClientState::Ready => {
    if c.is_alive().await { "running" } else { "dead" }
}
```

### Fix Plan (1-line change)

In `crates/mcp/src/sse_transport.rs`, change `is_alive()` to treat **any HTTP response** as proof the server is alive. Only network errors (connection refused, timeout, DNS failure) should mean "dead":

```rust
async fn is_alive(&self) -> bool {
    // ... existing request setup ...
    match req.send().await {
        Ok(resp) => {
            self.store_session_id_from_response(&resp).await;
            // Any HTTP response proves the server is reachable.
            // Non-2xx statuses (401, 403, 405) mean auth/method issues,
            // not that the server is dead. Only network errors = dead.
            true
        },
        Err(_) => false,
    }
}
```

After applying the fix, update the 3 Rust test assertions:
- `test_is_alive_405_method_not_allowed_returns_true` → `assert!(transport.is_alive().await)`
- `test_is_alive_403_forbidden_returns_true` → `assert!(transport.is_alive().await)`
- `test_is_alive_400_bad_request_returns_true` → `assert!(transport.is_alive().await)`

And update the E2E test assertion:
- `expect(statusText).toBe("running")` instead of `expect(["running", "dead"]).toContain(statusText)`

### Files Created/Modified

| File | What |
|------|------|
| `crates/web/ui/e2e/mock-mcp-server.js` | **New** — Mock MCP Streamable HTTP server for testing. Supports Bearer token auth, returns 405 for GET (reproduces #732). Implements `initialize`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`. |
| `crates/mcp/src/sse_transport.rs` | **Modified** — Added 5 `is_alive()` unit tests (200, 401, 405, 403, 400). The 405/403/400 tests assert the current buggy behavior with comments to flip after fix. |
| `crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/mcp.spec.js` | **Modified** — Added E2E test that starts mock MCP server, adds it via Streamable HTTP form with Bearer token, verifies status and tool count. |

### MCP Servers for Integration Testing

1. **`mock-mcp-server.js`** (created above) — Minimal Node.js mock, perfect for E2E tests. Configurable Bearer token and GET response status.

2. **`@modelcontextprotocol/server-everything`** — Official MCP test server from the MCP SDK. Provides many tools/resources. Can be used as `npx @modelcontextprotocol/server-everything` (stdio transport). Good for broader integration testing.

3. **`mockito` (Rust)** — Already used in the crate's tests. Best for unit-level transport testing (as done above). Flexible HTTP response mocking.

4. **Any public MCP server** — e.g., `https://mcp.linear.app/mcp` (already in featured list). Not suitable for automated tests but useful for manual QA.

 const { expect, test } = require("../base-test");
-const { navigateAndWait, watchPageErrors } = require("../helpers");
+const { navigateAndWait, watchPageErrors, waitForWsConnected } = require("../helpers");
+const { fork } = require("node:child_process");
+const path = require("node:path");
+
+/**
+ * Start the mock MCP Streamable HTTP server as a child process.
+ * Returns { port, process } — caller must kill the process on teardown.
+ */
+function startMockMcpServer(args = []) {
+	return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
+		var serverPath = path.resolve(__dirname, "../mock-mcp-server.js");
+		var child = fork(serverPath, args, { silent: true });
+		var output = "";
+
+		child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => {
+			output += chunk.toString();
+			try {
+				var parsed = JSON.parse(output.trim());
+				if (parsed.port) {
+					resolve({ port: parsed.port, process: child });
+				}
+			} catch {
+				// Not complete JSON yet, keep accumulating
+			}
+		});
+
+		child.on("error", reject);
+		child.on("exit", (code) => {
+			if (!output) reject(new Error(`Mock MCP server exited with code ${code}`));
+		});
+
+		setTimeout(() => reject(new Error("Mock MCP server startup timeout")), 5000);
+	});
+}

+

The server is alive.\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_is_alive_403_forbidden_returns_true() {\n        let mut server = mockito::Server::new_async().await;\n        let _mock = server\n            .mock(\"GET\", \"/\")\n            .with_status(403)\n            .create_async()\n            .await;\n\n        let transport = SseTransport::new(&server.url()).unwrap();\n        // BUG (issue #732): currently returns false.\n        assert!(\n            !transport.is_alive().await,\n            \"expected false (unfixed bug #732); flip to assert!(true) when fixed\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    /// A 400 Bad Request still proves the server endpoint is alive.\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_is_alive_400_bad_request_returns_true() {\n        let mut server = mockito::Server::new_async().await;\n        let _mock = server\n            .mock(\"GET\", \"/\")\n            .with_status(400)\n            .with_body(r#\"{\"error\":\"bad request\"}\"#)\n            .create_async()\n            .await;\n\n        let transport = SseTransport::new(&server.url()).unwrap();\n        // BUG (issue #732): currently returns false.\n        assert!(\n            !transport.is_alive().await,\n            \"expected false (unfixed bug #732); flip to assert!(true) when fixed\"\n        );",

```json
{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(mcp): treat any HTTP response as alive in health check (#732)\n\nSseTransport::is_alive() only considered 2xx and 401 responses as\nproof the server is alive. Streamable HTTP servers commonly return\n405 (GET is optional), 403, or 400 for the GET health probe, causing\na false \"dead\" status in the UI despite the server working fine.\n\nNow any HTTP response counts as alive — only network errors\n(connection refused, timeout, DNS failure) mean the server is dead.\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit the fix"
}
```

}

[{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P1\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **E2E assertion doesn't reproduce the bug**\n\n`expect([\"running\", \"dead\"]).toContain(statusText)` accepts any valid status value, so this assertion passes regardless of whether bug #732 is present or already fixed. It can never fail, which means it won't prompt the fix-PR author to update it either.\n\nThe Rust unit tests in this same PR take the correct approach: they assert the *current buggy behavior* (`assert!(!transport.is_alive().await)`) with a comment to flip after fix. The E2E test should be consistent:\n\n```suggestion\n\t\t\t// BUG #732: Currently shows \"dead\". After fix, change to:\n\t\t\t// expect(statusText).toBe(\"running\");\n\t\t\texpect(statusText).toBe(\"dead\");\n```","id":3089366471,"line":null,"path":"crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/mcp.spec.js"},{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P2\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **Misleading test names for bug-asserting tests**\n\n`test_is_alive_405_method_not_allowed_returns_true`, `test_is_alive_403_forbidden_returns_true`, and `test_is_alive_400_bad_request_returns_true` all say `_returns_true`, but their current assertions check for `false` (the bug). A reader seeing only the function name gets the opposite impression of what the test is doing right now.\n\nConsider naming them to reflect current behavior, e.g. `test_is_alive_405_currently_false_bug_732`, or add a `_bug_732` suffix so the inconsistency is self-documenting at the function-name level.","id":3089366553,"line":949,"path":"crates/mcp/src/sse_transport.rs"},{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P2\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **`setTimeout` handle never cleared**\n\nThe timeout fires 5 seconds after the call regardless of whether the promise already resolved. Calling `reject` on an already-settled promise is harmless in JS, but the lingering timer delays garbage collection of the child process reference and can produce confusing timing noise in test runners. Clear it on resolution:\n\n```suggestion\n\t\tvar timeoutHandle = setTimeout(() =\u003e reject(new Error(\"Mock MCP server startup timeout\")), 5000);\n\t\tchild.stdout.on(\"data\", (chunk) =\u003e {\n\t\t\toutput += chunk.toString();\n\t\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t\tvar parsed = JSON.parse(output.trim());\n\t\t\t\tif (parsed.port) {\n\t\t\t\t\tclearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n\t\t\t\t\tresolve({ port: parsed.port, process: child });\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t} catch {\n\t\t\t\t// Not complete JSON yet, keep accumulating\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t});\n```","id":3089366620,"line":33,"path":"crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/mcp.spec.js"},{"body":"\u003ca href=\"#\"\u003e\u003cimg alt=\"P2\" src=\"https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=7\" align=\"top\"\u003e\u003c/a\u003e **`stderr` not captured — startup errors silently dropped**\n\n`fork(..., { silent: true })` suppresses the child's stderr from the parent console, but there is no `child.stderr.on(\"data\", ...)` listener. If the mock server fails to start (e.g. missing `node:http`, syntax error), the error output is lost and CI will time out with \"Mock MCP server startup timeout\" rather than the actual cause.\n\nAdd a `stderr` listener to surface failures:\n```js\nchild.stderr.on(\"data\", (chunk) =\u003e {\n    process.stderr.write(`[mock-mcp-server] ${chunk}`);\n});\n```","id":3089366697,"line":13,"path":"crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/mcp.spec.js"}]

  "new_string": "function startMockMcpServer(args = []) {\n\treturn new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n\t\tvar serverPath = path.resolve(__dirname, \"../mock-mcp-server.js\");\n\t\tvar child = fork(serverPath, args, { silent: true });\n\t\tvar output = \"\";\n\t\tvar timeoutHandle = setTimeout(() => reject(new Error(\"Mock MCP server startup timeout\")), 5000);\n\n\t\tchild.stderr.on(\"data\", (chunk) => {\n\t\t\tprocess.stderr.write(`[mock-mcp-server] ${chunk}`);\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tchild.stdout.on(\"data\", (chunk) => {\n\t\t\toutput += chunk.toString();\n\t\t\ttry {\n\t\t\t\tvar parsed = JSON.parse(output.trim());\n\t\t\t\tif (parsed.port) {\n\t\t\t\t\tclearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n\t\t\t\t\tresolve({ port: parsed.port, process: child });\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t} catch {\n\t\t\t\t// Not complete JSON yet, keep accumulating\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t});\n\n\t\tchild.on(\"error\", (err) => {\n\t\t\tclearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n\t\t\treject(err);\n\t\t});\n\t\tchild.on(\"exit\", (code) => {\n\t\t\tif (!output) {\n\t\t\t\tclearTimeout(timeoutHandle);\n\t\t\t\treject(new Error(`Mock MCP server exited with code ${code}`));\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t});\n\t});\n}"

<a href="#"><img alt="P1" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p1.svg?v=7" align="top"></a> **Flaky status assertion — no retry on status transition**

`await expect(stateBadge).toBeVisible()` resolves as soon as a span matching `running|dead|stopped|connecting` appears — if the status starts as `"connecting"` while `is_alive()` is in flight, the locator snaps to that span and the following `stateBadge.textContent()` call reads `"connecting"` synchronously, failing the assertion without ever waiting for `"running"`. Playwright's `toHaveText` retries automatically; `textContent()` does not.

```suggestion
			var stateBadge = serverEntry.locator("span").filter({ hasText: /^(running|dead|stopped|connecting)$/ }).first();
			await expect(stateBadge).toHaveText("running", { timeout: 15_000 });
```
```

> AGENT

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/penso/.superset/worktrees/moltis/zesty-variety/crates/web/ui/e2e/specs/mcp.spec.js",
  "old_string": "\t\t\t// Check the state badge text — should be \"running\" now that #732 is fixed\n\t\t\tvar stateBadge = serverEntry.locator(\"span\").filter({ hasText: /^(running|dead|stopped|connecting)$/ }).first();\n\t\t\tawait expect(stateBadge).toBeVisible();\n\t\t\texpect(await stateBadge.textContent()).toBe(\"running\");",
  "new_string": "\t\t\t// Check the state badge text — should be \"running\" now that #732 is fixed.\n\t\t\t// Use toHaveText with retry to avoid flakes during status transitions.\n\t\t\tvar stateBadge = serverEntry.locator(\"span\").filter({ hasText: /^(running|dead|stopped|connecting)$/ }).first();\n\t\t\tawait expect(stateBadge).toHaveText(\"running\", { timeout: 15_000 });"
}
